The agent that translates Sovereign vision into sprint-executable decisions with BoK anchors.\n\n### 2. Core Capabilities\n- Product strategy (Phase roadmap: Beta \u2192 Commercial \u2192 Growth \u2192 Scale)\n- Product discovery (validating assumptions with Usu\u00e1rio Zero before Phase Commercial gates)\n- Roadmap planning (sprint sequencing by leverage \u00d7 BoK coverage \u00d7 RPN risk)\n- Product metrics (M3 retention as north star, mcoCoins burn rate as engagement proxy)\n- Go-to-market strategy (Usu\u00e1rio 1 acquisition hypothesis, BRL pricing validation, PT-BR positioning)\n- Product lifecycle management (feature backlog \u2192 sprint \u2192 production \u2192 sunsetting decision)\n- Product economics (mcoCoins cost calibration, plan tier pricing, Stripe readiness gating)\n- Stakeholder communication (translating engineering reality back to Sovereign vision language)\n\n### 3. Skills (Operational Level)\n- Prioritize sprint backlog items by leverage \u00d7 BoK readiness \u00d7 FMEA RPN score\n- Validate mcoCoins pricing against value delivered (10 coins/run = what creator hour-savings equivalent?)\n- Design Phase Commercial gate criteria (what must be definitively true before Stripe live keys flip?)\n- Map `sprint-priorities.md` gap closures to product outcomes (not just technical debt resolution)\n- Define Usu\u00e1rio 1 acquisition hypothesis: which persona converts first and why?\n- Monitor BO-003 mcoCoins burn rate (60-80% of plan quota) as the primary engagement health signal\n- Sequence LGPD compliance features (DD-001, DD-002) as hard Phase Commercial blockers\n- Distinguish between features serving Usu\u00e1rio Zero validation vs. features required for Usu\u00e1rio 1 conversion\n\n### 4. Inputs\n- `sprint-priorities.md` Gap Closure list, Sprint Goals, and 4Cs audit scores\n- HANDOFF.md Task State (sealed vs. in-progress phases, Pending Actions)\n- `pipeline_runs` (engagement signal \u2014 runs per creator per week, step success rates)\n- `03-prd.md` personas and journey maps (Mateus, Renata, Camilo goals and frustrations)\n- `02-brd.md` business objectives (BO-001..BO-005 with SMART metrics)\n- All 8 agent outputs (technical feasibility, security gates, UX signals, process health)\n\n### 5. Outputs\n- Sprint goal proposals grounded in BoK (not just engineering backlog)\n- Phase Commercial readiness report (go/no-go dashboard for Stripe live + Usu\u00e1rio 1 onboarding)\n- mcoCoins economics analysis (plan tier sustainability modeling at 10/50/100 account scale)\n- Feature prioritization recommendations with BoK gate status per item\n- Product narrative section updates for `03-prd.md` and `02-brd.md` enrichment\n\n### 6. Decision Scope\n- **Autonomous recommendation:** Sprint priority ordering (which gap closure has highest leverage \u00d7 BoK coverage)\n- **Autonomous recommendation:** Whether a new feature request belongs to current phase or should be deferred\n- **Autonomous recommendation:** Whether the mcoCoins cost per run needs recalibration based on LLM cost trends\n- **Human-in-loop (Sovereign):** Pricing tier changes (Starter/Pro/Enterprise BRL amounts)\n- **Human-in-loop (Sovereign):** Phase Commercial launch timing (Stripe live flip + first client outreach decision)\n- **Human-in-loop (Sovereign):** Build vs. buy vs. defer for new capability categories\n\n### 7. Tools & Integrations\n- `pipeline_runs` Supabase table (usage telemetry for product-level decisions)\n- `subscriptions` Supabase table (conversion and retention data \u2014 when live)\n- `sprint-priorities.md` (backlog source of truth and sprint velocity history)\n- MCORCH BoK Scribe Agent (`/bok-scribe`) for product documentation generation\n- `mcorch_nodes` Knowledge Mesh (product decisions as `decision` type nodes, semantically retrievable)\n- Stripe Dashboard (subscription analytics and cohort analysis \u2014 when live keys active)\n\n### 8. KPIs / Success Metrics\n- Phase Commercial gate: DD-001 + DD-002 + Stripe live + Usu\u00e1rio 1 onboarded (BO-001, \u2264 2026-Q3)\n- M3 retention proxy on Usu\u00e1rio Zero: \u2265 10 pipeline runs in month 3 (habit formation validation)\n- mcoCoins burn rate: 60-80% of plan quota per active account per month (BO-003 engagement signal)\n- Sprint velocity: \u2265 1 sealed gap closure per sprint (leverage \u00d7 BoK discipline maintained)\n- BoK gate compliance: 0 features shipped without FR anchor (co-metric with BABOK Agent)\n\n### 9. Example Use Cases\n- **Phase Commercial Readiness Synthesis** \u2014 ProdBOK Agent consolidates signals from all 7 other agents into a single Phase Commercial checklist: LGPD export/deletion (CISSP + SWEBOK), data classification (DMBOK), onboarding flow (CXBOK), pipeline reliability \u2265 98% (BPM CBOK), Stripe live security (CISSP), Usu\u00e1rio 1 FR coverage (BABOK). Sovereign sees a single go/no-go dashboard.\n- **mcoCoins Pricing Validation** \u2014 Usu\u00e1rio Zero ran 47 pipelines in 4 weeks (470 coins consumed). Starter plan (500 coins) barely covers month-one usage for an active creator. ProdBOK Agent models three scenarios: increase Starter to 800 coins, keep 500 and accept upgrade prompt trigger at 90% burn, or offer 200 bonus coins on first run. Recommends option 3 as the highest conversion-to-habit driver with lowest revenue risk.\n- **Twitter API Upgrade Decision** \u2014 Twitter Basic tier ($100/mo) unlocks auto-publish on X. ProdBOK Agent weighs: current external revenue (R$0), Twitter audience of Usu\u00e1rio Zero, LinkedIn-only publishing sufficiency for Phase Commercial entry. Recommends deferring until BO-001 (first external account converts) \u2014 LinkedIn alone is sufficient to validate the commercial proposition.\n\n### 10. Collaboration Map\n\u2192 BABOK: Translates product decisions into BoK requirements (new feature = new FRs before coding)\n\u2192 CXBOK: Receives creator experience signals that inform priority (UX friction = product debt with retention impact)\n\u2192 BPM CBOK: Monitors pipeline process health as product reliability proxy (reliability = M3 retention)\n\u2192 CISSP CBK: Receives security requirements that gate product phases (LGPD = Phase Commercial hard stop)\n\u2192 EABOK: Evaluates architectural feasibility before committing roadmap items to sprint planning\n\n---\n\n## \ud83d\udd04 Orchestration Model\n\nThe system is **event-driven with ProdBOK as the sprint-cycle orchestrator**. No agent runs continuously \u2014 each activates in response to specific triggers in the MCORCH development lifecycle.\n\n**Primary Orchestrator:** ProdBOK Agent \u2014 governs agent activation sequencing per sprint cycle and Phase gate decisions.\n\n### Trigger Conditions\n\n| Trigger | Activates |\n|---------|-----------|\n| New feature request from Sovereign | BABOK (BoK gate check) \u2192 ProdBOK (sprint placement) |\n| New migration scaffolded | CISSP CBK (FMEA-011 security review) + DMBOK (schema governance) |\n| New edge function scaffolded | CISSP CBK (JWT/CORS audit) + EABOK (architecture review) |\n| Pipeline failure rate drops below 95% | BPM CBOK (bottleneck analysis) \u2192 SWEBOK (fix implementation) |\n| Embedding coverage drops below 95% | DMBOK (backfill trigger via `backfill-embeddings.ts`) |\n| Sprint planning | ProdBOK (prioritization) \u2192 BABOK (BoK gate status per item) |\n| Phase gate transition (Commercial) | All 8 agents produce readiness reports \u2192 Sovereign go/no-go |\n| New UX regression detected | CXBOK (audit) \u2192 SWEBOK (fix) \u2192 CXBOK (re-verify) |\n\n### Key Handoff Points\n1. **BABOK \u2192 SWEBOK:** Validated FR spec with test ID anchors \u2192 implementation begins\n2. **CISSP CBK \u2192 SWEBOK:** Security code requirements \u2192 specific implementation patterns enforced\n3. **DMBOK \u2192 SWEBOK:** Validated schema \u2192 hook implementation uses correct column names and RLS assumptions\n4. **BPM CBOK \u2192 SWEBOK:** Retry/idempotency specifications \u2192 edge function implementation\n5. **CXBOK \u2192 SWEBOK:** UX implementation specs \u2192 component + toast + loading state patterns\n6. **SWEBOK \u2192 DMBOK:** New migration \u2192 data governance + security review before `db push`\n7. **All agents \u2192 ProdBOK:** Phase readiness signals aggregated \u2192 go/no-go decision for Sovereign\n\n### Feedback Loops\n- `pipeline_runs` telemetry \u2192 BPM CBOK (process performance) \u2192 SWEBOK (implementation fixes) \u2192 improved `pipeline_runs` success rate\n- `mcorch_nodes` growth \u2192 DMBOK (embedding quality audit) \u2192 `embed-mcorch-node` backfill trigger \u2192 restored coverage\n- AIOS `aios_conversations` \u2192 CXBOK (friction signal) \u2192 SWEBOK (UX fix) \u2192 CXBOK (re-audit)\n- FMEA RPN scores \u2192 CISSP CBK / SWEBOK (risk remediation) \u2192 `08-quality-metrics.md` update \u2192 lower RPN\n\n### Conflict Resolution\n- **SWEBOK vs. CISSP CBK (security slows shipping):** CISSP CBK is a hard gate \u2014 the security review is non-negotiable. SWEBOK adapts the implementation approach, not the security requirement.\n- **BABOK vs. ProdBOK (BoK documentation vs. shipping velocity):** The MCORCH Master Execution Protocol is explicit \u2014 no code without documented FRs. ProdBOK adjusts sprint timing but cannot bypass the BoK gate.\n- **EABOK vs. SWEBOK (architecture purity vs. pragmatic implementation):** Deviations are documented as Architecture Debt nodes in `mcorch_nodes` \u2014 not silently ignored, not blocking shipping if the risk is bounded and acknowledged.\n\n---\n\n## \ud83e\udde0 Intelligent Engineering Layer\n\n| Dimension | MCORCH Constellation Implementation |\n|-----------|--------------------------------------|\n| **People** | Gabriel Zarattini (Sovereign) is augmented by all 8 agents \u2014 each handles domain intelligence that would otherwise require a dedicated specialist. The system is designed for a solo founder operating at engineering team capacity. As the team grows from 1 to 5+, agents hand off domain ownership to human specialists rather than being replaced \u2014 they become the institutional memory and quality gates. |\n| **Process** | Agents embed into the MCORCH development cadence via the ShakeHands protocol (`/handson` = context pickup, `/handoff` = session seal with milestone node). Every sprint follows: BABOK gate check \u2192 SWEBOK implementation \u2192 CISSP/DMBOK review \u2192 BPM CBOK process validation \u2192 CXBOK UX audit \u2192 ProdBOK seal. The `sprint-priorities.md` + `HANDOFF.md` pair is the shared process state that persists across session boundaries. |\n| **Technology** | Required infrastructure: Supabase (Postgres + RLS + pgvector HNSW + Edge Functions), Docker Sovereign Mesh (mcorch_chroma, mcorch_claude_mem, mega-brain-daemon/webhook/dashboard), Claude Code (terminal + Claude Sonnet 4.6 as primary execution model), GitNexus MCP (AST code graph analysis), GitHub MCP (repository operations). All agent outputs are persisted to `mcorch_nodes` for semantic retrievability via AIOS. |\n| **Data** | The Knowledge Mesh (`mcorch_nodes` / `mcorch_edges`) is the shared intelligence layer \u2014 every agent decision, ADR, FMEA finding, process observation, and session handoff becomes a searchable node with 768d HNSW embeddings. FSRS-6 stability decay ensures high-signal nodes surface above stale ones. The mesh compounds: decisions made 6 months ago are semantically retrievable by AIOS context injection without manual briefing. |\n| **Knowledge** | Agents learn through structured documentation updates (BoK enrichment cycles) and mesh node insertion. The 9-doc BoK suite is the knowledge backbone; the dynamic mesh is the real-time layer. AIOS (Gemini Flash + Claude Sonnet 4.6) consumes both via `useKnowledgeMesh.fetchLatestContext()` \u2014 the `[MESH_STATE]` context string provides agents with current system knowledge at session start without requiring manual briefing of session history. |\n| **Value** | Value flows from agent activation to Sovereign time savings. A BABOK gate check that prevents a week of rework on an undocumented feature is worth more than any single feature shipped. BPM CBOK reliability improvements translate directly to M3 creator retention (BO-002). CISSP CBK security gates prevent LGPD incidents that would end Phase Commercial before it starts. Value is measured in leverage (hours saved \u00d7 risk eliminated), not lines of code. |\n| **Sustainable Impact** | (1) *Environmental:* Supabase managed infrastructure scales without dedicated server overhead; ChromaDB runs locally via Docker without cloud vector API costs; no over-provisioning at Usu\u00e1rio Zero scale. (2) *Social:* LGPD-first design protects Brazilian creator data sovereignty; PT-BR native generation respects creator language; mcoCoins democratizes AI access at R$147/month for solo creators who cannot afford enterprise AI tooling. (3) *Economic:* The compound knowledge mesh creates a moat that grows with every run \u2014 making the creator's subscription more valuable over time, not less. |\n\n---\n\n## \u2699\ufe0f Cross-Cutting Enablers\n\n### 1. Systems Thinking\nThe agent ecosystem models MCORCH as an adaptive feedback system with explicit feedback loops and hard gates. Each agent monitors one loop: BABOK (requirements \u2194 implementation), DMBOK (data quality \u2194 mesh density), BPM CBOK (process specs \u2194 runtime metrics), CISSP CBK (security design \u2194 threat surface), CXBOK (UX design \u2194 creator friction), EABOK (architecture intent \u2194 implementation drift), SWEBOK (implementation correctness \u2194 test coverage), ProdBOK (strategy \u2194 velocity). When one loop degrades, it surfaces to ProdBOK for cross-agent coordination. Interdependencies are explicit as Knowledge Mesh edges (REQUIRES, CALLS, DERIVES_FROM) \u2014 not assumed or implicit.\n\n### 2. Analytics & Metrics\nThe shared observability layer is Supabase: `pipeline_runs` (process + product metrics at step level), `mcorch_nodes` (knowledge growth + embedding health), `infra_health_logs` (infrastructure container health), `aios_conversations` (UX signal from creator natural language), `subscriptions` (business health \u2014 active when Stripe live). All agents read from and write to this shared telemetry fabric. No agent has a private metric store \u2014 all metrics are mesh-accessible, AIOS-queryable via `[MESH_STATE]`, and visible in the dashboard KPI panels. The `08-quality-metrics.md \u00a72` KPI Dashboard is the source of truth for system-level health across all 8 agent domains.\n\n### 3. Collaboration & Communication\nInter-agent communication happens through structured document updates (BoK enrichment), mesh node insertions (ADR and milestone nodes in `mcorch_nodes`), and the ShakeHands protocol (`HANDOFF.md` as durable shared state). Human-agent collaboration happens through the AIOS terminal (Gemini Flash + Claude Sonnet 4.6 with mesh context via `fetchLatestContext`), `/handson` (session pickup with full HANDOFF + sprint context), and `/handoff` (session seal with commit + milestone node + `sprint-priorities.md` update). The `[MESH_STATE]` header injected into every AIOS session ensures all agents share the same current system knowledge without manual briefing. Asynchronous signals (FMEA updates, doc-debt items, sprint gap closures) are written to persistent files that survive session boundaries and are semantically queryable.\n\n### 4. Automation & Tooling\nThe automation infrastructure runs four persistent cron jobs: watchdog `*/5 * * * *`, auto-publish `*/5 * * * *`, audit-4cs `0 12 * * 1` (Monday 09:00 BRT), and AST ingest `0 6 * * *` \u2014 all operating without agent intervention under normal conditions. Agent-triggered automation uses: `embed-mcorch-node` (embedding on every `mcorch_nodes` INSERT via Postgres trigger), `rescue-video` (recovery on Veo operation timeout), `refresh-social-token` (OAuth token refresh before expiry). The pre-commit hook (`scripts/pre-commit-check.sh`) is agent-enforced automation that runs transparently and catches the security failures CISSP CBK would otherwise detect manually. `harness-deploy.sh` is the single-command deploy surface that eliminates per-service deployment cognitive overhead for the Sovereign.\n\n### 5. Ethics, Compliance & Sustainability\nLGPD is a system-level constraint applied by every agent, not a CISSP CBK-only concern. BABOK requires LGPD BRs for any data-collecting feature. DMBOK classifies PII in every new table schema. EABOK enforces RLS-by-default as an architectural invariant. SWEBOK implements SECURITY DEFINER RPCs for financial operations. CISSP CBK audits the implementation against LGPD Articles 17-22. CXBOK designs consent-clear data collection flows with transparent PT-BR language. ProdBOK gates Phase Commercial on DD-001 + DD-002 completion. Sustainability means: the system is designed to be operated by one person indefinitely without heroics, and creator data sovereignty is a foundational design value \u2014 not a compliance checkbox added at the end.\n\n---\n\n\u2192 ALIGN  \u2192  INTEGRATE  \u2192  OPTIMIZE  \u2192  INNOVATE  \u2192  TRANSFORM  \u2192  DELIVER VALUE\n\n**SYSTEM STATUS: ALL SYSTEMS OPERATIONAL**\n"
}

  "content": "-- Migration: 20260508025933_delete_account_rpc\n-- Description: LGPD Article 17 self-service account deletion RPC (DD-001 / BR-018)\n-- Security Checklist (FMEA-011):\n--   [x] RLS not required (SECURITY DEFINER function \u2014 operates as service role)\n--   [x] Default deny via REVOKE/GRANT pattern below\n--   [x] No PII columns exposed \u2014 function deletes PII\n--   [x] No secrets hardcoded\n--   [x] /security-review approved\n\n-- delete_account: cascade-deletes all user-owned data, returns affected table summary\n-- SECURITY DEFINER ensures the function runs with owner privileges, bypassing RLS\n-- Client MUST pass their own user_id; edge function verifies auth.uid() == p_user_id\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.delete_account(p_user_id uuid)\nRETURNS jsonb\nLANGUAGE plpgsql\nSECURITY DEFINER\nSET search_path = public\nAS $$\nDECLARE\n  v_result jsonb;\n  v_content_count int;\n  v_runs_count int;\n  v_nodes_count int;\n  v_conversations_count int;\n  v_social_count int;\n  v_scheduled_count int;\nBEGIN\n  -- Verify caller is deleting their own account (belt-and-suspenders; edge fn also checks)\n  IF auth.uid() IS NOT NULL AND auth.uid() != p_user_id THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'Unauthorized: cannot delete another user''s account';\n  END IF;\n\n  -- Count rows before deletion for audit summary\n  SELECT COUNT(*) INTO v_content_count      FROM content_library    WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  SELECT COUNT(*) INTO v_runs_count         FROM pipeline_runs      WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  SELECT COUNT(*) INTO v_nodes_count        FROM mcorch_nodes       WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  SELECT COUNT(*) INTO v_conversations_count FROM aios_conversations WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  SELECT COUNT(*) INTO v_social_count       FROM social_accounts    WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  SELECT COUNT(*) INTO v_scheduled_count    FROM scheduled_posts    WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n\n  -- Cascade delete in dependency order (child tables first)\n  DELETE FROM scheduled_posts    WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  DELETE FROM social_accounts    WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  DELETE FROM aios_conversations WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  DELETE FROM mcorch_edges       WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  DELETE FROM mcorch_nodes       WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  DELETE FROM pipeline_runs      WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  DELETE FROM content_library    WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  DELETE FROM user_api_keys      WHERE user_id = p_user_id;\n  DELETE FROM profiles           WHERE id = p_user_id;\n\n  v_result := jsonb_build_object(\n    'deleted', true,\n    'user_id', p_user_id,\n    'tables_affected', jsonb_build_object(\n      'content_library',    v_content_count,\n      'pipeline_runs',      v_runs_count,\n      'mcorch_nodes',       v_nodes_count,\n      'aios_conversations', v_conversations_count,\n      'social_accounts',    v_social_count,\n      'scheduled_posts',    v_scheduled_count\n    )\n  );\n\n  RETURN v_result;\nEND;\n$$;\n\n-- Only authenticated users can call this function (edge fn further restricts to own user_id)\nREVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.delete_account(uuid) FROM PUBLIC;\nGRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.delete_account(uuid) TO authenticated;\nGRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.delete_account(uuid) TO service_role;\n"
}

  "content": "import { createClient } from \"https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2.49.1\";\n\nconst corsHeaders = {\n  \"Access-Control-Allow-Origin\": \"*\",\n  \"Access-Control-Allow-Methods\": \"POST, OPTIONS\",\n  \"Access-Control-Allow-Headers\":\n    \"authorization, x-client-info, apikey, content-type\",\n};\n\nDeno.serve(async (req) => {\n  if (req.method === \"OPTIONS\") return new Response(\"ok\", { headers: corsHeaders });\n\n  const authHeader = req.headers.get(\"Authorization\");\n  if (!authHeader?.startsWith(\"Bearer \")) {\n    return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: \"Unauthorized\" }), {\n      status: 401, headers: { ...corsHeaders, \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n    });\n  }\n\n  try {\n    const supabaseUrl = Deno.env.get(\"SUPABASE_URL\")!;\n    const serviceKey = Deno.env.get(\"SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY\")!;\n\n    const userClient = createClient(supabaseUrl, Deno.env.get(\"SUPABASE_ANON_KEY\")!, {\n      global: { headers: { Authorization: authHeader } },\n    });\n    const { data: { user }, error: authError } = await userClient.auth.getUser();\n    if (authError || !user) {\n      return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: \"Unauthorized\" }), {\n        status: 401, headers: { ...corsHeaders, \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n      });\n    }\n\n    const admin = createClient(supabaseUrl, serviceKey);\n\n    // Collect all PII tables for this user (LGPD Article 20 \u2014 right to data portability)\n    const [profile, content, runs, nodes, conversations, social, scheduled] = await Promise.all([\n      admin.from(\"profiles\").select(\"*\").eq(\"id\", user.id).maybeSingle(),\n      admin.from(\"content_library\").select(\"id, type, title, body, status, created_at, metadata\").eq(\"user_id\", user.id),\n      admin.from(\"pipeline_runs\").select(\"id, topic, platforms, status, steps, started_at, completed_at\").eq(\"user_id\", user.id),\n      admin.from(\"mcorch_nodes\").select(\"id, node_type, name, content, stability_score, created_at\").eq(\"user_id\", user.id),\n      admin.from(\"aios_conversations\").select(\"id, title, created_at, updated_at\").eq(\"user_id\", user.id),\n      admin.from(\"social_accounts\").select(\"id, platform, username, status, created_at\").eq(\"user_id\", user.id),\n      admin.from(\"scheduled_posts\").select(\"id, platform, scheduled_at, status, created_at\").eq(\"user_id\", user.id),\n    ]);\n\n    // Log export request to infra_health_logs (service_role write)\n    await admin.from(\"infra_health_logs\").insert({\n      service: \"data-export\",\n      status: \"healthy\",\n      last_seen_at: new Date().toISOString(),\n    }).then(() => {});\n\n    const exportPayload = {\n      exported_at: new Date().toISOString(),\n      user_id: user.id,\n      email: user.email,\n      mcorch_version: \"5.8.0\",\n      lgpd_notice: \"Exporta\u00e7\u00e3o de dados pessoais conforme LGPD Art. 18 e 20. Dados v\u00e1lidos no momento da exporta\u00e7\u00e3o.\",\n      tables: {\n        profile: profile.data ?? null,\n        content_library: content.data ?? [],\n        pipeline_runs: runs.data ?? [],\n        knowledge_nodes: nodes.data ?? [],\n        aios_conversations: conversations.data ?? [],\n        social_accounts: social.data ?? [],\n        scheduled_posts: scheduled.data ?? [],\n      },\n    };\n\n    const filename = `mcorch-data-export-${new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)}.json`;\n    const body = JSON.stringify(exportPayload, null, 2);\n\n    return new Response(body, {\n      headers: {\n        ...corsHeaders,\n        \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\",\n        \"Content-Disposition\": `attachment; filename=\"${filename}\"`,\n      },\n    });\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(\"[data-export] error:\", e);\n    return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : \"Unknown error\" }), {\n      status: 500, headers: { ...corsHeaders, \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n    });\n  }\n});\n"
}

// Log deletion request BEFORE deleting (user won't exist after)\n    console.log(`[delete-account] User ${user.id} (${user.email}) initiated account deletion`);\n    await admin.from(\"infra_health_logs\").insert({\n      service: \"delete-account\",\n      status: \"healthy\",\n      last_seen_at: new Date().toISOString(),\n    }).then(() => {});\n\n    // Cascade console.error(\"[delete-account] RPC error:\", rpcError);\n      return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: \"Falha ao excluir dados da conta\" }), {\n        status: 500, headers: { ...corsHeaders, \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n      });\n    }\n\n    // Delete the auth.users record (requires service role admin API)\n    const { error: authDeleteError } = await admin.auth.admin.deleteUser(user.id);\n    if (authDeleteError) {\n      console.error(\"[delete-account] auth.admin.deleteUser error:\", authDeleteError);\n      // Data is already deleted; log but don't block the response\n    }\n\n    return new Response(JSON.stringify({ ...deleteResult, auth_deleted: !authDeleteError }), {\n      headers: { ...corsHeaders, \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n    });\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(\"[delete-account] error:\", e);\n    return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : \"Unknown   "content": "import { useState } from \"react\";\nimport { supabase } from \"@/integrations/supabase/client\";\nimport { toast } from \"sonner\";\n\nexport function useDataExport() {\n  const [isExporting, setIsExporting] = useState(false);\n\n  const exportData = async () => {\n    setIsExporting(true);\n    try {\n      const { data: { session } } = await supabase.auth.getSession();\n      if (!session) {\n        toast.error(\"Sess\u00e3o expirada. Fa\u00e7a login novamente.\");\n        return;\n      }\n\n      const supabaseUrl = import.meta.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL as string;\n      const res = await fetch(`${supabaseUrl}/functions/v1/data-export`, {\n        method: \"POST\",\n        headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${session.access_token}` },\n      });\n\n      if (!res.ok) {\n        const err = await res.json().catch(() => ({ error: \"Erro ao exportar dados\" }));\n        toast.error(err.error ?? \"Erro ao exportar dados\");\n        return;\n      }\n\n      const blob = await res.blob();\n      const filename = res.headers.get(\"Content-Disposition\")?.match(/filename=\"(.+)\"/)?.[1]\n        ?? `mcorch-data-export-${new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)}.json`;\n\n      const url = URL.createObjectURL(blob);\n      const a = document.createElement(\"a\");\n      a.href = url;\n      a.download = filename;\n      a.click();\n      URL.revokeObjectURL(url);\n\n      toast.success(\"Exporta\u00e7\u00e3o conclu\u00edda. Verifique seus downloads.\");\n    } catch (e) {\n      console.error(\"[useDataExport]\", e);\n      toast.error(\"Erro ao exportar dados. Tente novamente.\");\n    } finally {\n      setIsExporting(false);\n    }\n  };\n\n  return { exportData, isExporting };\n}\n"
}

Tente novamente.\");\n        return false;\n      }\n\n      // Sign out locally \u2014 auth record is deleted server-side\n      await supabase.auth.signOut();\n      toast.success(\"Conta exclu\u00edda com sucesso. Tente novamente.\");\n      return false;\n    } finally {\n      setIsDeleting(false);\n    }\n  };\n\n  return { deleteAccount, isDeleting };\n}\n"
}

```

## PHASE 1 — SECURITY AUDIT

Run these checks and report findings. **Block the seal if any CRITICAL finding exists.**

> ⚠️ **SCOPE NOTE:** This phase scans changes that exist NOW (before Phase 4 writes HANDOFF.md).
> A second mandatory scan runs in Phase 4b, after HANDOFF.md is written and before it is committed.

```bash
# 1a. Secret leak scan — check staged + working tree for hardcoded credentials
git diff HEAD | grep -E "(sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{20,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}|eyJ[a-zA-Z0-9._-]{20,}|STRIPE_|SECRET_KEY|ACCESS_TOKEN|api_key\s*=\s*['\"][^'\"]{10,})" \
  | grep -v "example\|placeholder\|<.*>\|your-key\|YOUR_" | head -20

# 1b. Edge function JWT enforcement — every user-facing function must verify JWT
grep -rL "Authorization\|jwt\|JWT\|service_role" supabase/functions/*/index.ts \
  | grep -v "get-infra-status\|watchdog" || echo "ALL FUNCTIONS: JWT enforced ✅"

# 1c. Client-side coin deduction guard — must NEVER update mco_balance directly from client
grep -rn "mco_balance.*update\|UPDATE.*mco_balance" src/ \
  | grep -v "settings\|top.up\|topup\|SettingsPage" | head -10 || echo "NO VIOLATIONS ✅"

# 1d. RLS bypass risk — check for supabase.rpc calls without auth context
grep -rn "service_role\|bypass.*rls\|rls.*bypass" src/ | head -10 || echo "NO VIOLATIONS ✅"

# 1e. Sensitive data in console.log
grep -rn "console\.log.*token\|console\.log.*key\|console\.log.*secret\|console\.log.*password" src/ \
  | head -10 || echo "NO SENSITIVE LOGS ✅"
```

**Security verdict:** list each check as ✅ PASS / ⚠️ WARN / 🔴 BLOCK.
Only proceed if no 🔴 findings.

---

### DOCUMENTATION CREDENTIAL RULE (invariant — never violate)

**When writing HANDOFF.md, README.md, or any committed documentation:**

- ✅ DO: describe WHERE a credential is stored → `VITE_GEMINI_API_KEY` is set in `.env` (local) and `GEMINI_API_KEY` in the Supabase vault
- ✅ DO: reference digest/fingerprint if useful → `GEMINI_API_KEY (digest 0fe0e159...)`
- 🔴 NEVER: include the actual credential value → `AIzaSyBv...`, `sk-...`, `eyJ...`

> **Rationale:** Phase 1 scans changes that predate the HANDOFF.md write. Any credential embedded
> in HANDOFF.md during Phase 4 escapes Phase 1 entirely and goes directly to the remote.
> This rule is the primary prevention layer; Phase 4b is the detection backstop.

## PHASE 4b — HANDOFF.md SECRET SCAN (mandatory before commit)

**Run this scan on the HANDOFF.md content about to be committed. Block if any finding exists.**

```bash
# 4b-1. Scan HANDOFF.md for real credential values
grep -nE "(AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}|sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{20,}|eyJ[a-zA-Z0-9._-]{40,}|ghp_[a-zA-Z0-9]{36}|xox[baprs]-[a-zA-Z0-9-]+|[a-zA-Z0-9]{32,}=\s*['\"]?[A-Za-z0-9+/]{40,})" HANDOFF.md \
  | grep -v "example\|placeholder\|<.*>\|your-key\|YOUR_\|digest\|sha256\|hash\|fingerprint" \
  | head -20 || echo "HANDOFF.md: NO CREDENTIALS FOUND ✅"

# 4b-2. Specifically check for Google API keys (AIza prefix = 39 chars total)
grep -n "AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]\{35\}" HANDOFF.md | head -10 || echo "NO GOOGLE API KEYS ✅"

# 4b-3. Check for any = "value" pattern that looks like a real assignment
grep -nE "=\s*['\"][A-Za-z0-9_\-]{20,}['\"]" HANDOFF.md \
  | grep -v "example\|placeholder\|<.*>\|your-\|YOUR_\|digest\|hash\|uuid\|id.*[0-9a-f-]\{36\}" \
  | head -10 || echo "NO INLINE ASSIGNMENTS ✅"
```

**If any check returns a match:**
1. 🔴 **DO NOT COMMIT** HANDOFF.md
2. Rewrite the offending section — replace the actual value with its location description
3. Re-run Phase 4b until all checks pass
4. Only then proceed to commit HANDOFF.md

Commit HANDOFF.md only after Phase 4b passes:
```
docs(handoff): seal <phase name> — <one-line summary>
```

   - Added `validateContent()` helper immediately after imports:
   ```typescript
   const CONTENT_MIN_LENGTH: Record<string, number> = { article: 500, linkedin: 50, twitter_thread: 30 };
   function validateContent(type: string, content: string): { valid: boolean; reason: string } {
     const trimmed = content.trim();
     if (!trimmed) return { valid: false, reason: "empty_content" };
     const min = CONTENT_MIN_LENGTH[type] ?? 30;
     if (trimmed.length < min) return { valid: false, reason: `too_short:${trimmed.length}/<${min}` };
     return { valid: true, reason: "ok" };
   }
   ```
   - Article section: validates after `args.content` parsed, clears `articleContent = ""` on failure (prevents WordPress step from running), calls `addStep("article_generation", "error", ...)`
   - LinkedIn section: validates `liText`, skips INSERT + schedule on failure
   - Twitter section: validates `tweets` array (also checks `tweets.length < 3`), uses `threadText` for content check

> 49	## 🎯 Overview
> 50	
> 51	**Constellation Orchestra** is a SaaS platform that transforms simple topics into fully monetized, multi-platform marketing campaigns. It acts as a **Harness** — a sovereign control mesh that orchestrates AI content generation, affiliate monetization (Mercado Livre), social media publishing, SEO optimization, real-time ROI tracking, and a conversational AIOS (Autonomous Intelligence Operating System).
> 52	
> 53	### Core Value Proposition
> 54	
> 55	```
> 56	1 Topic → 1 SEO Article (WordPress) + 1 B2B Post (LinkedIn) + 1 Thread (X/Twitter)
> 57	         + Affiliate Links (Mercado Livre) with per-campaign UTM attribution
> 58	         + handle-ml-postback → revenue_cents updated on every ML conversion
> 59	         + ROI Dashboard with real BRL revenue
> 60	         + GraphRAG Knowledge Node (observation inserted per run)
> 61	         + Neural HUD telemetry
> 62	         + Constellation Matrix WebGL (2095 nodes · 2543 edges — paginação completa)
> 63	         + Visual Orchestration Canvas with Quality Gates
> 64	         + AIOS — conversational AI consciousness with VAD + Barge-in
> 65	```
> 66	
> 67	### [v5.7.0] — BoK Suite v1 + A2UI Wiring + Test Coverage 122 (2026-05-07)
> 68	
> 69	- **BoK Scribe selado** — primeira família BoK completa do projeto em `docs/bok/mcorch-constellation/`: 9 documentos (`00-index` + MRD/BRD/PRD/FRD/SDD + Data Model + Process Flow + Quality Metrics) reverse-engineered do sistema rodando, ~29.185 palavras grounded em 177 src files, 30 migrations, 39 edge functions, 4 crons, 2564 nós e 122 testes. Single Source of Truth para futuras invocações de `/bok-agents-generator` e gate documental do MCORCH Master Execution Protocol.
> 70	- **Mesh Seal** — milestone node `bok-scribe-seal-mcorch-constellation-v1` (type `documentation_suite`, stability 0.85) inserido em `mcorch_nodes` via SQL editor + embedding automático via `trg_mcorch_embed_on_insert`. BoK agora é semanticamente recuperável pelo AIOS.
> 71	- **A2UI live em produção** — `parseAgentMessage` (de `useA2UI`) finalmente conectado ao callback `onMessage` do `AIOSTerminal`. Toda resposta do AIOS contendo um bloco JSON com `type:"a2ui"` agora spawna superfícies dinâmicas (MetricsPanel, AgentStatusCard, SystemAlert, TextBlock) na coluna central da CoreV2Page sem intervenção manual. Última peça pendente do Phase Zeta fechada.
> 72	- **Test coverage 66 → 122** — três hooks críticos passam de zero cobertura a suítes completas: `useOrchestrationGraph` (21 testes — runPipeline state transitions, mcoCoins gate, partial errors, persistPositions), `useKnowledgeMesh` (19 testes — multi-bucket retrieval, dedup, contextString, realtime channel, RLS-only tenant isolation), `useSubscription` (16 testes — Stripe checkout/portal, error paths, loading state). Padrão de mocking via `vi.hoisted` + `vi.mock` segue `phase-zeta.test.tsx`.
> 73	
> 74	### [v5.6.1] — Layout + UX Regression Fixes (2026-05-06)
> 75	
> 76	- **Layout regression** — `MatrixPage` e `MemoryPage` usavam `absolute inset-0` escaping o flex tree do `DashboardLayout` e sobrepondo sidebar e header. Corrigido para `relative w-full h-full overflow-hidden` (padrão `ConstellationPage`). Botões "voltar" redundantes removidos — breadcrumbs do layout cobrem navegação.
> 77	- **Search bar alinhada** — barra de busca da Matrix movida de `absolute left-1/2 -translate-x-1/2` (centralizada no canvas) para item inline no flex row dos badges. Dropdown convertido para `absolute top-full` — sem overlap com hub-node badge nem com o botão de auth.
> 78	- **Texto 100% opaco** — classes `text-*/NN` em `ConstellationMatrix`, `MeshControlPanel` e `MemoryPage` convertidas para opacidade total: `/35 /50 /60 /70 /30` removidos dos textos legíveis.
> 79	- **MeshControl sincronizado** — `Sovereign Mesh Control` agora exibe `nodeCount`/`edgeCount` do hook `useConstellationSigma` (mesmo que o HUD superior) em vez dos totais do banco via `trigger-ingest`. 2433 nós e 2564 arestas no painel e no header — valores idênticos.
> 80

> 64	## BoK Suite v1 + A2UI Wiring + Test Coverage 122 Record (2026-05-07)
> 65	
> 66	Sessão de fechamento de três grandes lacunas no AIOS de desenvolvimento, executadas em sequência sem rebuild ou interrupção do pipeline. Primeiro: o protocolo A2UI estava pronto há semanas mas faltava o último fio — a função `parseAgentMessage` definida em `useA2UI` nunca havia sido conectada ao callback de mensagem completada do `AIOSTerminal`. Toda resposta do agente que continha um bloco JSON com `type:"a2ui"` era silenciosamente descartada. A correção foi minimalista: adicionar `onMessage?: (content: string) => void` em `AIOSTerminalProps`, disparar `onMessage?.(ttsBufferRef.current)` no `useEffect` de fim de streaming antes do reset do buffer, e passar `parseAgentMessage` como `onMessage` do `<AIOSTerminal>` na `CoreV2Page`. Três linhas em dois arquivos. A2UI agora spawna superfícies dinâmicas (MetricsPanel, AgentStatusCard, SystemAlert, TextBlock) na coluna central da CoreV2Page automaticamente, sem intervenção manual. Segundo: cobertura de testes pulou de 66 para 122 com três suítes novas. `useOrchestrationGraph.test.ts` (21 tests) cobre runPipeline em todos os caminhos críticos — input vazio, transições mid-flight, sucesso completo com mapeamento step→nó, gate de mcoCoins (HTTP 402 → todos nós em `error`), erros parciais (gate-audit `error` → publishers `blocked`), e persistência de posições no localStorage. `useKnowledgeMesh.test.ts` (19 tests) valida `fetchLatestContext` multi-bucket com 7 priority types, dedup por ID, formato exato do `contextString` (header `[MESH_STATE]` + linhas `[TYPE] name (stability:X.XX): content[:200]`), `contextNodeIds`, `meshStats` com totalNodes/totalEdges/dominantTypes, garantia de que RLS isola tenants sem bypass de `user_id`, e ciclo de vida do canal Realtime (subscribe → unmount → removeChannel). `useSubscription.test.ts` (16 tests) cobre `checkout` sem user (toast + zero invoke), com user (Stripe redirect via `window.location.href`), error paths em `checkout` e `openPortal`, e transições de loading state. Padrão de mocking via `vi.hoisted` + `vi.mock` espelhando `phase-zeta.test.tsx`. Terceiro: o BoK Scribe Agent foi invocado em modo reverse-engineering para gerar a primeira família documental completa do projeto — 9 markdowns em `docs/bok/mcorch-constellation/` totalizando ~29.185 palavras, todas grounded em artefatos reais (177 src files, 30 migrations, 39 edge functions, 4 crons, 2564 nós, 122 testes). O BoK não inventa features: cada MR/BR/PR/FR cita um arquivo, função, tabela, RPC ou seção do HANDOFF. Onde o sistema rodando contradiz documentação prévia, o sistema vence — contradições viram doc-debt em `08-quality-metrics.md` (17 itens enumerados, 3 marcados ACTION REQUIRED por RPN > 100). O nó `bok-scribe-seal-mcorch-constellation-v1` (type `documentation_suite`, stability 0.85) foi inserido em `mcorch_nodes` via SQL editor do Supabase pelo Sovereign — embedding automático via `trg_mcorch_embed_on_insert`, BoK semanticamente recuperável pelo AIOS. Esta suíte é input obrigatório para futuras invocações de `/bok-agents-generator` (Phase 3 do plano BoK→Agents).

> 162	## Layout + UX Regression Fixes Record (2026-05-06)
> 163	
> 164	Sessão de correção de regressões visuais acumuladas após o DevOps + Security Seal. Cinco problemas identificados e resolvidos: layout de páginas full-canvas sobrepondo o sidebar, barra de busca conflitando com botão de auth, textos com opacidade reduzida dificultando leitura, e contagem de nós do painel Sovereign Mesh Control dessincronizada do HUD superior. Ponto de diagnóstico: `MatrixPage` e `MemoryPage` usavam `absolute inset-0` como container raiz — classe que posiciona relativo ao ancestral posicionado mais próximo (não o `<main>` do DashboardLayout), escapando do flex tree e sobrepondo sidebar e header. O padrão correto (`relative w-full h-full overflow-hidden`), já adotado por `ConstellationPage`, foi aplicado às duas páginas. Como consequência direta, a search bar (que estava em `absolute top-3 left-1/2 -translate-x-1/2`) passou a ficar alinhada dentro do canvas — mas `left-1/2` do canvas com sidebar visível coincide com o fim dos badges. Solução: mover a search bar para dentro do flex row dos badges como item inline (`relative w-64`), com dropdown convertido de fluxo normal para `absolute top-full` para não empurrar outros badges. Para textos opacos: varredura em `ConstellationMatrix`, `MeshControlPanel` e `MemoryPage` — todas as classes `text-*/NN` com sufixo de opacidade em elementos de texto legível foram convertidas para variantes full-opacity. `MeshControlPanel` agora recebe `edgeCount` como prop adicional e exibe `nodeCount`/`edgeCount` do hook `useConstellationSigma` em vez dos totais do banco via `trigger-ingest`, garantindo paridade com o HUD superior.
